Critical

CISA KEV: CVE-2025-49704 — Microsoft SharePoint Code Injection Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2025-07-22 6 use cases 1 technique 5 kill-chain phases detected

Microsoft SharePoint contains an improper authentication vulnerability that allows an authorized attacker to perform spoofing over a network. Successfully exploitation could allow an attacker to view sensitive information and make some changes to disclosed information. This vulnerability could be chained with CVE-2025-49704. CVE-2025-53771 is a patch bypass for CVE-2025-49706, and the updates for CVE-2025-53771 include more robust protection than those for CVE-2025-49706. Vendor: Microsoft, Product: SharePoint. Known ransomware use: Known. Federal patch due: 2025-07-23.

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code.
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change · LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) · Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement
Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-49706", "CVE-2025-49704", "CVE-2025-53770", "CVE-2025-53771")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-49706", "CVE-2025-49704", "CVE-2025-53770", "CVE-2025-53771")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

| `drop_dm_object_name(Vulnerabilities)`
| sort - severity

    from datamodel=Vulnerabilities

Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) Actions on Objectives High
Mimikatz, comsvcs.dll MiniDump, or any non-Windows process opening LSASS.
ATT&CKT1003.001 T1003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceEvents
DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: LSASS Process Access Outside Microsoft-Signed Tooling
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d04
status: experimental
description: |
    Anything opening lsass.exe with read-memory access that isn't a
    Microsoft signed component (Defender, csrss, etc.) is suspect.
    Mimikatz / Pypykatz / nanodump signature.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1003/001/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1003.001
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: process_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection:
        TargetImage|endswith: '\lsass.exe'
    filter_known_good:
        SourceImage|endswith:
            - '\MsSense.exe'
            - '\MsMpEng.exe'
            - '\csrss.exe'
            - '\svchost.exe'
            - '\wininit.exe'
            - '\services.exe'
            - '\lsm.exe'
            - '\SearchProtocolHost.exe'
    condition: selection and not filter_known_good
falsepositives:
    - Third-party EDR / forensic tools — extend the filter list
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where (Processes.process="*lsass*" OR Processes.process="*sekurlsa*"
        OR Processes.process="*MiniDump*" OR Processes.process="*comsvcs.dll*MiniDump*"
        OR Processes.process="*procdump*lsass*")
       OR (Processes.process_name="rundll32.exe" AND Processes.process="*comsvcs*MiniDump*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where (Processes.process="*lsass*" OR Processes.process="*sekurlsa*"
        OR Processes.process="*MiniDump*" OR Processes.process="*comsvcs.dll*MiniDump*"
        OR Processes.process="*procdump*lsass*")
       OR (Processes.process_name="rundll32.exe" AND Processes.process="*comsvcs*MiniDump*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement Actions on Objectives High
psexec / paexec / smbexec / wmic /node — service install over SMB from remote host.
ATT&CKT1021.002 T1569.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
   or (FileName =~ "wmic.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has "/node:")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by Timestamp desc

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688            // process-create (audit-policy required)
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\psexec.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\psexesvc.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\paexec.exe"
   or (NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe" and CommandLine has "/node:")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: PsExec / SMB Lateral Movement Tooling Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d03
status: experimental
description: |
    PsExec, paexec, smbexec, csexec, remcom — admin-grade tooling that
    pivots over SMB. Pair with `wmic /node:` for the full cluster.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1021/002/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1569/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1021.002
    - attack.t1569.002
    - attack.lateral-movement
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_psexec:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\psexec.exe'
            - '\psexec64.exe'
            - '\psexesvc.exe'
            - '\paexec.exe'
            - '\csexec.exe'
            - '\remcom.exe'
            - '\smbexec.py'
    selection_wmic_remote:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\wmic.exe'
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '/node:'
    condition: selection_psexec or selection_wmic_remote
falsepositives:
    - Sysadmins running approved remote-management tooling — wrap with
      a known-admin-host watchlist
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
       OR (Processes.process_name="wmic.exe" AND Processes.process="*/node:*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
       OR (Processes.process_name="wmic.exe" AND Processes.process="*/node:*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where DstIpAddr in (BadIPs)
   or Url has_any (BadDomains)
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber, Url, NetworkProtocol
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Outbound Connection to Article-Named IP / Domain
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d0f
status: experimental
description: |
    Replace `__IP_LIST__` / `__DOMAIN_LIST__` placeholders with
    article-specific destinations. For dynamic IOC management, use a
    watchlist or external feed.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/tactics/TA0011/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.command-and-control
logsource:
    category: network_connection
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_ip:
        DestinationIp:
            - '__IP_PLACEHOLDER_1__'
            - '__IP_PLACEHOLDER_2__'
    selection_domain:
        DestinationHostname:
            - '__DOMAIN_PLACEHOLDER_1__'
            - '__DOMAIN_PLACEHOLDER_2__'
    condition: selection_ip or selection_domain
falsepositives:
    - None on a true-positive IOC match
level: critical

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"

// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.

union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: File or Process Matching Article Hash IOCs
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d0b
status: experimental
description: |
    Replace `__HASH_LIST__` placeholder with article-specific SHA256
    values. Use a watchlist or external indicator file for management
    at scale.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1027/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1027
    - attack.defense-evasion
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection:
        Hashes|contains:
            - 'SHA256=__HASH_PLACEHOLDER_REPLACE_ME__'
    condition: selection
falsepositives:
    - None on a true-positive hash match
level: critical
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

Cursor IDE Malware Extension Compromise in $500k Crypto Heist

Snyk
2025-07-21 12 use cases 3 techniques 6 kill-chain phases detected

Learn about the recent $500K crypto heist caused by a malicious "Solidity Language" extension in Cursor IDE. Understand how compromised IDE extensions and third-party registries pose significant supply chain risks for developers and the broader AI ecosystem.

Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] Cursor IDE or VS Code spawning PowerShell/WScript from extensions folder (Solidity Language malware chain) · PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — Cursor IDE Malware Extension Compromise in $500k Crypto Heist

Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs
[LLM] Cursor IDE or VS Code spawning PowerShell/WScript from extensions folder (Solidity Language malware chain) Exploitation High
Detects Cursor.exe or Code.exe spawning interpreters (powershell.exe/wscript.exe/cscript.exe/cmd.exe) where the child command line references the Kaspersky-confirmed C2 hosts angelic.su, lmfao.su, staketree.net, paste.ee or ab498.pythonanywhere.com, or where the parent chain is rooted in the .cursor\extensions tree. Anchors directly on the Solidity Language extension.js → PowerShell pivot Kaspersky documented. Rationale: Anchored on Kaspersky-documented chain: Cursor.exe loads `%userprofile%\.cursor\extensions\solidityai.solidity-1.0.9-universal\src\extension.js` which spawns PowerShell to fetch angelic.su/files/1.txt and 2.txt, then staketree.net for the npm-side variant. The combination of IDE parent + scripting child + named C2 host is the high-fidelity tuple. Confirmed by Kaspersky Securelist, Kaspersky corporate blog, and BleepingComputer. Cross-checked against: • https://securelist.com/open-source-package-for-cursor-ai-turned-into-a-crypto-heist/116908/ • https://www.kaspersky.com/blog/malicious-extensions-for-cursor-ai/53802/ • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/malicious-vscode-extension-in-cursor-ide-led-to-500k-crypto-theft/
ATT&CKT1059.001 T1195.002 T1204.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(14d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("Cursor.exe","Code.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","cmd.exe")
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("angelic.su","lmfao.su","staketree.net","paste.ee/d/","ab498.pythonanywhere.com")
   or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any (@"\.cursor\extensions\", @"\.vscode\extensions\")
   or InitiatingProcessFolderPath has_any (@"\.cursor\", @"\.vscode\")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          ParentImage = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ChildImage = FolderPath,
          ChildCmd = ProcessCommandLine,
          SHA256, InitiatingProcessSHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(14d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName endswith "\\Cursor.exe" or ParentProcessName endswith "\\Code.exe"
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\\powershell.exe","\\pwsh.exe","\\wscript.exe","\\cscript.exe","\\cmd.exe")
| where CommandLine has_any ("angelic.su","lmfao.su","staketree.net","paste.ee/d/","ab498.pythonanywhere.com",@".cursor\extensions",@".vscode\extensions")
| where SubjectUserName !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, SubjectUserName, ParentProcessName, NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Cursor or VS Code IDE Spawning Interpreter With Solidity Language Malware C2
id: 9f3d4a1b-7c2e-4f08-b3a4-1d0e8e5a2c61
description: Cursor.exe or Code.exe spawning a script interpreter whose command line references C2 hosts confirmed in the Kaspersky Solidity Language Open VSX malware investigation (angelic.su / lmfao.su / staketree.net / paste.ee / ab498.pythonanywhere.com) or originates from the extensions install tree.
references:
  - https://securelist.com/open-source-package-for-cursor-ai-turned-into-a-crypto-heist/116908/
  - https://www.kaspersky.com/blog/malicious-extensions-for-cursor-ai/53802/
  - https://snyk.io/blog/cursor-ide-malware-extension-compromise-in-usd500k-crypto-heist/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/21
tags:
  - attack.t1059.001

logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_parent:
    ParentImage|endswith:
      - '\Cursor.exe'
      - '\Code.exe'
  selection_child:
    Image|endswith:
      - '\powershell.exe'
      - '\pwsh.exe'
      - '\wscript.exe'
      - '\cscript.exe'
      - '\cmd.exe'
  selection_indicator:
    CommandLine|contains:
      - 'angelic.su'
      - 'lmfao.su'
      - 'staketree.net'
      - 'paste.ee/d/'
      - 'ab498.pythonanywhere.com'
      - '.cursor\\extensions'
      - '.vscode\\extensions'
  condition: selection_parent and selection_child and selection_indicator
level: high
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:1 @ParentImage:(*\\Cursor.exe OR *\\cursor.exe OR *\\Code.exe OR *\\code.exe) @Image:(*\\powershell.exe OR *\\PowerShell.exe OR *\\pwsh.exe OR *\\wscript.exe OR *\\WScript.exe OR *\\cscript.exe OR *\\cmd.exe) (@CommandLine:(*angelic.su* OR *lmfao.su* OR *staketree.net* OR *paste.ee/d/* OR *ab498.pythonanywhere.com*) OR @ParentCommandLine:(*\\.cursor\\extensions\\* OR *\\.vscode\\extensions\\*))
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 ParentBaseFileName=/^(Cursor|Code)\.exe$/i ImageFileName=/(powershell|pwsh|wscript|cscript|cmd)\.exe$/i
| regex("(angelic\\.su|lmfao\\.su|staketree\\.net|paste\\.ee/d/|ab498\\.pythonanywhere\\.com|\\\\.cursor\\\\extensions\\\\|\\\\.vscode\\\\extensions\\\\)", field=CommandLine)
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ParentBaseFileName, ImageFileName, CommandLine], function=count())

[LLM] Egress to Solidity Language Cursor extension C2 infrastructure (angelic.su / lmfao.su / staketree.net / ab498.pythonanywhere.com / 144.172.1 Command & Control High
Network connections or DNS lookups to the C2 hosts Kaspersky published for the Solidity Language Open VSX extension campaign that stole $500K. Direct IOC alert — these domains have no legitimate business use. Rationale: All five hostnames and the IP 144.172.112.84 are vendor-confirmed atomic IOCs from the Kaspersky Securelist write-up. No legitimate enterprise use case for `lmfao.su` or `angelic.su`, and `ab498.pythonanywhere.com` is a single-tenant PythonAnywhere C2 the same actor reused. IP corroborated as the resolver for relay.lmfao.su. Cross-checked against: • https://securelist.com/open-source-package-for-cursor-ai-turned-into-a-crypto-heist/116908/ • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/malicious-vscode-extension-in-cursor-ide-led-to-500k-crypto-theft/ • https://advisory.eventussecurity.com/advisory/fake-solidity-extension-delivers-malware-to-blockchain-developers/
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1568
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Web.Web Network_Resolution.DNS
let badHosts = dynamic(["angelic.su","relay.lmfao.su","lmfao.su","staketree.net","ab498.pythonanywhere.com"]);
let badIps = dynamic(["144.172.112.84"]);
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteUrl has_any (badHosts) or RemoteIP in (badIps) or RemoteUrl endswith ".angelic.su" or RemoteUrl endswith ".lmfao.su" or RemoteUrl endswith ".staketree.net"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Protocol
| order by Timestamp desc

[LLM] Solidity Language malicious Cursor/VS Code extension folder created on disk (solidityai.solidity-* and related) Delivery High
File-event detection for the installation of any extension folder Kaspersky tied to this campaign — solidityai.solidity-*, solaibot, among-eth, blankebesxstnion, pythonformat, C++ Playground, HTTP Format — under %userprofile%\.cursor\extensions\ or %userprofile%\.vscode\extensions\, and especially when extension.js is written inside. Rationale: Kaspersky identifies the exact extension folder name `%userprofile%\.cursor\extensions\solidityai.solidity-1.0.9-universal\src\extension.js` and links sister VS Code marketplace extensions `solaibot`, `among-eth`, `blankebesxstnion` to the same actor by shared C2. Folder-name match is a near-zero-FP signal because no legitimate publisher uses these IDs. [LLM] ScreenConnect MSI sideload from lmfao.su (Solidity Language post-exploit RAT install) Installation High
Detects the second-stage MSI install Kaspersky documented — msiexec or curl/Invoke-WebRequest pulling ScreenConnect.ClientSetup.msi from lmfao.su with Access/Guest URL parameters, then ScreenConnect.ClientService.exe joining relay.lmfao.su. This is the threat actor's RAT-of-choice for follow-on hands-on-keyboard activity. Rationale: The URL `https://lmfao.su/Bin/ScreenConnect.ClientSetup.msi?e=Access&y=Guest` is the exact second-stage RAT installer Kaspersky captured. The query-string `?e=Access&y=Guest` is a ScreenConnect-relay-build artefact unique to this actor — it ties any host that runs the installer to this campaign, even on rebuilt SCC binaries. Cross-checked against: • https://securelist.com/open-source-package-for-cursor-ai-turned-into-a-crypto-heist/116908/ • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/malicious-vscode-extension-in-cursor-ide-led-to-500k-crypto-theft/
ATT&CKT1219 T1105
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Web.Web
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FileName in~ ("msiexec.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","curl.exe","certutil.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| where ProcessCommandLine has "lmfao.su"
   or ProcessCommandLine has "ScreenConnect.ClientSetup.msi" and ProcessCommandLine has_any ("e=Access&y=Guest","lmfao")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          ParentName = InitiatingProcessFileName, ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where NewProcessName endswith_cs "\\msiexec.exe" or NewProcessName endswith_cs "\\powershell.exe" or NewProcessName endswith_cs "\\pwsh.exe" or NewProcessName endswith_cs "\\curl.exe" or NewProcessName endswith_cs "\\certutil.exe" or NewProcessName endswith_cs "\\bitsadmin.exe"
| where CommandLine has "lmfao.su" or (CommandLine has "ScreenConnect.ClientSetup.msi" and CommandLine has_any ("e=Access&y=Guest","lmfao"))
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, SubjectUserName, ParentProcessName, NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1555/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'
            - '\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\'
    selection_file:
        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'
            - '\cookies.sqlite'
    filter_browser:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'
            - '\msedge.exe'
            - '\firefox.exe'
            - '\brave.exe'
            - '\opera.exe'
    condition: selection_path and selection_file and not filter_browser
falsepositives:
    - Backup utilities reading user profile files
    - Browser sync agents from non-stock vendors
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command Exploitation High
Encoded or obfuscated PowerShell — common across loaders, recon, and post-exploitation.
ATT&CKT1059.001 T1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(-enc|encodedcommand|frombase64string|-nop|-w\s+hidden|invoke-expression|iex\s*\(|downloadstring|net\.webclient)"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\pwsh.exe"
| where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(-enc|encodedcommand|frombase64string|-nop|-w\s+hidden|invoke-expression|iex\s*\(|downloadstring|net\.webclient)"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName,
          CommandLine, ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Obfuscated / Encoded PowerShell Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d08
status: experimental
description: |
    PowerShell launched with -EncodedCommand, base64 strings, hidden
    window, no-profile, bypass policy, IEX / Invoke-Expression / Net
    .WebClient.DownloadString — combinations indicating evasion.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1027/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059.001
    - attack.t1027
    - attack.execution
    - attack.defense-evasion
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_image:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
    selection_args:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '-enc '
            - 'EncodedCommand'
            - 'FromBase64String'
            - 'Invoke-Expression'
            - 'IEX('
            - 'DownloadString'
            - 'Net.WebClient'
            - '-w hidden'
            - '-WindowStyle Hidden'
            - '-nop'
            - '-NoProfile'
            - '-ep bypass'
            - '-ExecutionPolicy Bypass'
    condition: selection_image and selection_args
falsepositives:
    - DevOps tooling that legitimately uses encoded command for
      cross-shell escaping
level: high

        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64String*" OR Processes.process="*-nop*"
        OR Processes.process="*-w hidden*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*IEX(*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*Net.WebClient*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
      AND (Processes.process="*-enc *" OR Processes.process="*EncodedCommand*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64String*" OR Processes.process="*-nop*"
        OR Processes.process="*-w hidden*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*IEX(*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*Net.WebClient*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents

// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine

        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"

union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Actions on Objectives
—
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code.
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
Critical

CISA KEV: CVE-2025-53770 — Microsoft SharePoint Deserialization of Untrusted Data Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2025-07-20 6 use cases 1 technique 5 kill-chain phases detected

Microsoft SharePoint Server on-premises contains a deserialization of untrusted data vulnerability that could allow an unauthorized attacker to execute code over a network. This vulnerability could be chained with CVE-2025-53771. CVE-2025-53770 is a patch bypass for CVE-2025-49704, and the updates for CVE-2025-53770 include more robust protection than those for CVE-2025-49704. Vendor: Microsoft, Product: SharePoint. Known ransomware use: Known. Federal patch due: 2025-07-21.

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code.
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change · LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) · Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement
Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-53770", "CVE-2025-53771", "CVE-2025-49704", "CVE-2025-49706")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-53770", "CVE-2025-53771", "CVE-2025-49704", "CVE-2025-49706")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) Actions on Objectives High
Mimikatz, comsvcs.dll MiniDump, or any non-Windows process opening LSASS.
ATT&CKT1003.001 T1003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceEvents
DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: LSASS Process Access Outside Microsoft-Signed Tooling
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d04
status: experimental
description: |
    Anything opening lsass.exe with read-memory access that isn't a
    Microsoft signed component (Defender, csrss, etc.) is suspect.
    Mimikatz / Pypykatz / nanodump signature.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1003/001/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1003.001
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: process_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection:
        TargetImage|endswith: '\lsass.exe'
    filter_known_good:
        SourceImage|endswith:
            - '\MsSense.exe'
            - '\MsMpEng.exe'
            - '\csrss.exe'
            - '\svchost.exe'
            - '\wininit.exe'
            - '\services.exe'
            - '\lsm.exe'
            - '\SearchProtocolHost.exe'
    condition: selection and not filter_known_good
falsepositives:
    - Third-party EDR / forensic tools — extend the filter list
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where (Processes.process="*lsass*" OR Processes.process="*sekurlsa*"
        OR Processes.process="*MiniDump*" OR Processes.process="*comsvcs.dll*MiniDump*"
        OR Processes.process="*procdump*lsass*")
       OR (Processes.process_name="rundll32.exe" AND Processes.process="*comsvcs*MiniDump*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where (Processes.process="*lsass*" OR Processes.process="*sekurlsa*"
        OR Processes.process="*MiniDump*" OR Processes.process="*comsvcs.dll*MiniDump*"
        OR Processes.process="*procdump*lsass*")
       OR (Processes.process_name="rundll32.exe" AND Processes.process="*comsvcs*MiniDump*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement Actions on Objectives High
psexec / paexec / smbexec / wmic /node — service install over SMB from remote host.
ATT&CKT1021.002 T1569.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
   or (FileName =~ "wmic.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has "/node:")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by Timestamp desc

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688            // process-create (audit-policy required)
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\psexec.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\psexesvc.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\paexec.exe"
   or (NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe" and CommandLine has "/node:")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: PsExec / SMB Lateral Movement Tooling Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d03
status: experimental
description: |
    PsExec, paexec, smbexec, csexec, remcom — admin-grade tooling that
    pivots over SMB. Pair with `wmic /node:` for the full cluster.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1021/002/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1569/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1021.002
    - attack.t1569.002
    - attack.lateral-movement
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_psexec:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\psexec.exe'
            - '\psexec64.exe'
            - '\psexesvc.exe'
            - '\paexec.exe'
            - '\csexec.exe'
            - '\remcom.exe'
            - '\smbexec.py'
    selection_wmic_remote:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\wmic.exe'
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '/node:'
    condition: selection_psexec or selection_wmic_remote
falsepositives:
    - Sysadmins running approved remote-management tooling — wrap with
      a known-admin-host watchlist
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
       OR (Processes.process_name="wmic.exe" AND Processes.process="*/node:*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
       OR (Processes.process_name="wmic.exe" AND Processes.process="*/node:*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
let BadIPs     = dynamic(["107.191.58.76", "104.238.159.149", "96.9.125.147", "134.199.202.205", "141.164.60.10", "131.226.2.6", "188.130.206.168", "65.38.121.198", "139.144.199.41", "89.46.223.88", "45.77.155.170", "154.223.19.106", "185.197.248.131", "149.40.50.15", "64.176.50.109", "149.28.124.70", "206.166.251.228", "95.179.158.42", "86.48.9.38", "128.199.240.182", "212.125.27.102", "91.132.95.60", "145.239.97.206"]);
let BadDomains = dynamic(["update.updatemicfosoft.com", "msupdate.updatemicfosoft.com", "c34718cbb4c6.ngrok-free.app", "bpp.theinnovationfactory.it", "ice.theinnovationfactory.it"]);
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where DstIpAddr in (BadIPs)
   or Url has_any (BadDomains)

| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Outbound Connection to Article-Named IP / Domain
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d0f
status: experimental
description: |
    Replace `__IP_LIST__` / `__DOMAIN_LIST__` placeholders with
    article-specific destinations. For dynamic IOC management, use a
    watchlist or external feed.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/tactics/TA0011/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.command-and-control
logsource:
    category: network_connection
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_ip:
        DestinationIp:
            - '__IP_PLACEHOLDER_1__'
            - '__IP_PLACEHOLDER_2__'
    selection_domain:
        DestinationHostname:
            - '__DOMAIN_PLACEHOLDER_1__'
            - '__DOMAIN_PLACEHOLDER_2__'
    condition: selection_ip or selection_domain
falsepositives:
    - None on a true-positive IOC match
level: critical

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)

    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,

title: File or Process Matching Article Hash IOCs
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d0b
status: experimental
description: |
    Replace `__HASH_LIST__` placeholder with article-specific SHA256
    values. Use a watchlist or external indicator file for management
    at scale.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1027/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1027
    - attack.defense-evasion
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection:
        Hashes|contains:
            - 'SHA256=__HASH_PLACEHOLDER_REPLACE_ME__'
    condition: selection
falsepositives:
    - None on a true-positive hash match
level: critical
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

2025-07-18 1 use case 0 techniques 2 kill-chain phases detected

Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).

ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-25257")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-25257")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("c637ec00bd22da4539ec6def89cd9f7196a303d17632b1131a89d65e4f5698f4", "f0fcc638cd93bdd6fb4745d75b491395a7a1b2cb08e0153a2eb417cb2f58d8ac") or SHA1 in~ ("c637ec00bd22da4539ec6def89cd9f7196a303d17632b1131a89d65e4f5698f4", "f0fcc638cd93bdd6fb4745d75b491395a7a1b2cb08e0153a2eb417cb2f58d8ac") or MD5 in~ ("c637ec00bd22da4539ec6def89cd9f7196a303d17632b1131a89d65e4f5698f4", "f0fcc638cd93bdd6fb4745d75b491395a7a1b2cb08e0153a2eb417cb2f58d8ac")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("c637ec00bd22da4539ec6def89cd9f7196a303d17632b1131a89d65e4f5698f4", "f0fcc638cd93bdd6fb4745d75b491395a7a1b2cb08e0153a2eb417cb2f58d8ac") or SHA1 in~ ("c637ec00bd22da4539ec6def89cd9f7196a303d17632b1131a89d65e4f5698f4", "f0fcc638cd93bdd6fb4745d75b491395a7a1b2cb08e0153a2eb417cb2f58d8ac") or MD5 in~ ("c637ec00bd22da4539ec6def89cd9f7196a303d17632b1131a89d65e4f5698f4", "f0fcc638cd93bdd6fb4745d75b491395a7a1b2cb08e0153a2eb417cb2f58d8ac")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["c637ec00bd22da4539ec6def89cd9f7196a303d17632b1131a89d65e4f5698f4", "f0fcc638cd93bdd6fb4745d75b491395a7a1b2cb08e0153a2eb417cb2f58d8ac"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

CISA KEV: CVE-2025-5777 — Citrix NetScaler ADC and Gateway Out-of-Bounds Read Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2025-07-10 4 use cases 2 techniques 3 kill-chain phases detected

Citrix NetScaler ADC and Gateway contain an out-of-bounds read vulnerability due to insufficient input validation. This vulnerability can lead to memory overread when the NetScaler is configured as a Gateway (VPN virtual server, ICA Proxy, CVPN, RDP Proxy) OR AAA virtual server. Vendor: Citrix, Product: NetScaler ADC and Gateway. Known ransomware use: Known. Federal patch due: 2025-07-11.

ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-5777")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-5777")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT

| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) Actions on Objectives High

ATT&CKT1003.001 T1003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceEvents
DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] GitHub Actions Runner.Worker memory dumped via /proc on Linux runner (reviewdog payload)
[LLM] Compromised reviewdog/action-setup commit SHA referenced in GitHub Actions workflow (CVE-2025-30154) Delivery High
Hunts for any workflow_run or repository content that references the specific malicious commit f0d342d24037bb11d26b9bd8496e0808ba32e9ec injected into reviewdog/action-setup@v1, or any unpinned reviewdog/action-setup@v1 execution during the March 11, 2025 18:42-20:31 UTC compromise window. Surfaces repos that may have leaked CI/CD secrets to publicly readable workflow logs. Rationale: Anchored on the named-IOC commit SHA f0d342d24037bb11d26b9bd8496e0808ba32e9ec (cited in StepSecurity + Wiz + the reviewdog GHSA advisory) and on the v1-tag dereference inside the precise 18:42-20:31 UTC compromise window from the maintainer's response. Defender is left empty because GitHub Actions audit telemetry is not in MDE; Sigma left empty because there is no canonical Sigma logsource for GitHub Actions audit. The malicious SHA does NOT overlap with the StepSecurity replacement fab6de28... which is the recovery pin recommended in the article. Cross-checked against: • https://www.wiz.io/blog/new-github-action-supply-chain-attack-reviewdog-action-setup • https://github.com/reviewdog/reviewdog/security/advisories/GHSA-qmg3-hpqr-gqvc • https://www.cvedetails.com/cve/CVE-2025-30154/ • https://github.com/reviewdog/action-setup/issues/54 • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/github-action-hack-likely-led-to-another-in-cascading-supply-chain-attack/
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1554 T1078.004
Data sourcesChange.All_Changes
// GitHub Actions audit logs typically land in a customer-specific custom table (e.g. GitHubAuditLogPolling_CL). Adapt the table name to your tenant.
let MaliciousSha = "f0d342d24037bb11d26b9bd8496e0808ba32e9ec";
let CompromiseStart = datetime(2025-03-11 18:42:00);
let CompromiseEnd   = datetime(2025-03-11 20:31:00);
union isfuzzy=true
  (GitHubAuditLogPolling_CL
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(90d)
    | where action_s in ("workflows.completed_workflow_run","workflows.created_workflow_run","workflows.prepared_workflow_job","repo.push")
    | extend Payload = tostring(parse_json(tostring(workflow_run_s)))
    | where Payload has "reviewdog/action-setup" or Payload has MaliciousSha),
  (GitHubAudit_CL
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(90d)
    | where tostring(pack_all()) has "reviewdog/action-setup" or tostring(pack_all()) has MaliciousSha)
| extend MaliciousShaSeen = iif(tostring(pack_all()) has MaliciousSha, true, false)
| extend V1InWindow = iif(tostring(pack_all()) has "reviewdog/action-setup@v1" and TimeGenerated between (CompromiseStart .. CompromiseEnd), true, false)
| where MaliciousShaSeen or V1InWindow
| project TimeGenerated, repo_s, actor_s, action_s, MaliciousShaSeen, V1InWindow
source:(github OR github.audit) (@workflow.steps.uses:*reviewdog/action-setup* OR @workflow_run.head_sha:f0d342d24037bb11d26b9bd8496e0808ba32e9ec OR @repo.content:*f0d342d24037bb11d26b9bd8496e0808ba32e9ec* OR (@workflow.steps.uses:"reviewdog/action-setup@v1" AND @timestamp:[2025-03-11T18:42:00Z TO 2025-03-11T20:31:00Z]))

| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Change.command) as command values(Change.object_attrs) as object_attrs values(Change.user) as user from datamodel=Change where (Change.object="reviewdog/action-setup" OR Change.command="*reviewdog/action-setup*" OR Change.object_attrs="*reviewdog/action-setup*") by Change.object Change.action _time span=1h | `drop_dm_object_name(Change)` | eval malicious_sha=if(match(object_attrs,"f0d342d24037bb11d26b9bd8496e0808ba32e9ec"),1,0) | eval v1_tag_in_window=if((match(object_attrs,"reviewdog/action-setup@v1($|[^a-f0-9])") OR match(command,"reviewdog/action-setup@v1($|[^a-f0-9])")) AND _time>=strptime("2025-03-11 18:42:00","%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S") AND _time<=strptime("2025-03-11 20:31:00","%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S"),1,0) | where malicious_sha=1 OR v1_tag_in_window=1 | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
[LLM] GitHub Actions Runner.Worker memory dumped via /proc on Linux runner (reviewdog payload) Actions on Objectives High
Detects the article's specific payload behaviour: a python3 process that enumerates /proc/<pid>/cmdline searching for 'Runner.Worker', then reads /proc/<pid>/maps and /proc/<pid>/mem to scrape decrypted GitHub Actions secrets from runner memory. Applies to self-hosted Linux runners with EDR/auditd coverage and is article-specific because of the Runner.Worker + /proc/*/mem combination. Rationale: Anchored to two unique strings copied straight from the article's decoded payload: the literal byte sequence 'Runner.Worker' that the script greps in /proc/<pid>/cmdline, and the /proc/<pid>/maps + /proc/<pid>/mem read pattern. Combining python3 + 'Runner.Worker' + /proc/.../mem is essentially zero-FP because no legitimate runner-side tooling dumps the Runner.Worker process address space. Cross-checked against Wiz's payload analysis and the reviewdog GHSA advisory (GHSA-qmg3-hpqr-gqvc / CVE-2025-30154) which corroborate the exact /proc-memory-scrape technique. Cross-checked against: • https://www.wiz.io/blog/new-github-action-supply-chain-attack-reviewdog-action-setup • https://github.com/reviewdog/reviewdog/security/advisories/GHSA-qmg3-hpqr-gqvc • https://www.cvedetails.com/cve/CVE-2025-30154/ • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/github-action-hack-likely-led-to-another-in-cascading-supply-chain-attack/
ATT&CKT1003.007 T1552.001 T1059.006 T1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
// Defender for Endpoint Linux on a self-hosted GitHub runner
let RunnerWorkerCue = dynamic(["Runner.Worker","/proc/","/maps","/mem","cmdline_f","map_path","mem_path"]);
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where InitiatingProcessVersionInfoProductName has "Linux" or FolderPath startswith "/usr/" or FolderPath startswith "/opt/" or FolderPath startswith "/home/"
| where FileName in~ ("python","python2","python3") or InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("python","python2","python3")
| where ProcessCommandLine has "Runner.Worker"
   or (ProcessCommandLine has "/proc/" and (ProcessCommandLine has "/maps" or ProcessCommandLine has "/mem"))
   or InitiatingProcessParentFileName =~ "Runner.Worker"
   or InitiatingProcessFileName =~ "Runner.Worker"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath,
          ProcessCommandLine,
          Parent = InitiatingProcessFileName,
          GrandParent = InitiatingProcessParentFileName,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
// ASIM normalised process telemetry from any Linux EDR / auditd connector
let WindowStart = ago(30d);
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > WindowStart
| where DvcOs has "Linux" or DvcOs has "linux"
| where TargetProcessName has_any ("python","python2","python3")
   or ActingProcessName has_any ("python","python2","python3")
| where TargetProcessCommandLine has "Runner.Worker"
   or (TargetProcessCommandLine has "/proc/" and (TargetProcessCommandLine has "/maps" or TargetProcessCommandLine has "/mem"))
   or ParentProcessName has "Runner.Worker"
   or ActingProcessName has "Runner.Worker"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername,
          TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine,
          ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine,
          ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, SHA256
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: GitHub Runner.Worker Process Memory Dump via /proc (reviewdog action-setup payload)
id: 9d7b2e44-3f0e-4a1a-9f12-7b8e1c4d5a91
status: experimental
description: Detects the malicious payload added to reviewdog/action-setup@v1 (commit f0d342d24037bb11d26b9bd8496e0808ba32e9ec) which uses python3 to scan /proc/<pid>/cmdline for the GitHub Actions Runner.Worker process and dump /proc/<pid>/maps + /proc/<pid>/mem to leak CI/CD secrets into the workflow log.
references:
    - https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/reviewdog-github-actions-are-compromised
    - https://www.wiz.io/blog/new-github-action-supply-chain-attack-reviewdog-action-setup
    - https://github.com/reviewdog/reviewdog/security/advisories/GHSA-qmg3-hpqr-gqvc
    - https://www.cvedetails.com/cve/CVE-2025-30154/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/13
tags:
    - attack.credential_access
    - attack.t1003.007
    - attack.t1552.001
    - attack.t1059.006
    - attack.t1195.002
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: linux
detection:
    selection_python:
        Image|endswith:
            - '/python'
            - '/python2'
            - '/python3'
    selection_runner_worker:
        CommandLine|contains: 'Runner.Worker'
    selection_proc_mem:
        CommandLine|contains|all:
            - '/proc/'
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '/maps'
            - '/mem'
    condition: selection_python and (selection_runner_worker or selection_proc_mem)
falsepositives:
    - Legitimate runner diagnostics scripts (very rare on production runners)
level: high
source:(linux.auditd OR linux.syslog) (@process.name:(python OR python2 OR python3) OR @process.executable.path:(*/python OR */python2 OR */python3)) (@process.command_line:*Runner.Worker* OR (@process.command_line:*/proc/* AND @process.command_line:(*/maps* OR */mem*)) OR @auditd.file.path:(/proc/*/mem OR /proc/*/maps))
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats summariesonly=true count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Processes.process) as process values(Processes.process_name) as process_name values(Processes.parent_process_name) as parent values(Processes.user) as user from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where Processes.os="Linux" Processes.process_name IN ("python","python2","python3") (Processes.process="*Runner.Worker*" OR (Processes.process="*/proc/*" AND Processes.process IN ("*/maps*","*/mem*")) OR Processes.parent_process_name="Runner.Worker" OR Processes.parent_process="*Runner.Worker*") by host Processes.dest Processes.process_id Processes.parent_process_id | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Processes.process) as process values(Processes.process_name) as process_name values(Processes.parent_process_name) as parent values(Processes.user) as user from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where Processes.os="Linux" Processes.process_name IN ("python","python2","python3") (Processes.process="*Runner.Worker*" OR (Processes.process="*/proc/*" AND Processes.process IN ("*/maps*","*/mem*")) OR Processes.parent_process_name="Runner.Worker" OR Processes.parent_process="*Runner.Worker*") by host Processes.dest Processes.process_id Processes.parent_process_id | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-30154", "CVE-2025-30066")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-30154", "CVE-2025-30066")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes

    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["f0d342d24037bb11d26b9bd8496e0808ba32e9ec", "b833eecdf13c615cd60d5dede6f6593a4b3b4376", "0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "6e6023c01918b353229af0881232f601a4cc8365", "f5434e31b6259b4e08684618a305bae127b6d784", "0f176b316e1d41a945e574fc2ba76b0dc752d585", "96be5a72d8adac89200e08658f69273912fe4783", "61902a2b3c982d3551ad219bb0ff22f3663e44de", "f966d8d897bc8033657b8e77da56a988029ce8c7", "909ace6b17fc4045030e55f5ac27ca99f276ae80", "454c8a19a12cde77505464d7e4549500c8ac68d0", "04d5b6d4c18c06d7df6edabf914d0ded986c3a87", "81796e43b6348d628e3e739a910d50704a5292c1", "8d73381aa1c2ccd12c8ddcfefa47aeb1443e67e3", "c27af8180030e1f3d0434473731f030dc1849edf", "efa6ce46bcaa8751ad223e44be7977798c909304", "143a52c0d919c1a69bdeafeab564650f6939a2b3", "31b1df0e735ad8511fd7df3be8cf9351d8cb4de7", "26f36301be817815fbcb896d2c85e89f04b17df4", "9bb460e92befdbb6506d2e643ae06c8b50205f97"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

CISA KEV: CVE-2019-5418 — Rails Ruby on Rails Path Traversal Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2025-07-07 1 use case 0 techniques 2 kill-chain phases detected

Rails Ruby on Rails contains a path traversal vulnerability in Action View. Specially crafted accept headers in combination with calls to `render file:` can cause arbitrary files on the target server to be rendered, disclosing the file contents. Vendor: Rails, Product: Ruby on Rails. Federal patch due: 2025-07-28.
